> **Moved out of `CLAUDE.md` on 2026-07-22, and this file is deliberately NOT auto-loaded.**
>
> `CLAUDE.md` is injected into every session in this repo **and into every subagent** — measured:
> one `Read` in a subagent re-injected it at **141,071 B ≈ 48.6k tokens**, on top of the ~311k-token
> always-loaded rules corpus. This backlog was **85,504 B — 61% of that file** — and it is *task
> state*, not instructions: nothing here changes how an agent approaches a problem, which is the
> test for what belongs in `CLAUDE.md`.

> `CLAUDE.md`'s own handoff rule already says **"Facts → the docs. Tasks → the Backlog."** This
> makes that literal.

> `B42`, `B45`, `B50`) as provenance for why a given gate exists, and those citations resolve here:
> most as open rows, and `B42` as a *closed* one recorded in the session-3 note below. A citation
> that lands on a closed row is still resolved — it tells you the gate's reason shipped.

## RUN CONTRACT — the walkthrough matrix (rows 1–6)

**This is the operator's standing order, written down because it has had to be re-issued five times.
Nothing in it is optional and nothing in it may be narrowed. A run that violates any line of it is
not a run — it is a harness exercise, and reporting it as a matrix result is the failure this
contract exists to stop.**

### 0. The order, verbatim

1. *"cut a new lab and do rows 1,2,3,4,5 until they green from the first time, while watchfully whole
   reading scenario-1 and scenario-2, executing your own instructions line by line, observing full and
   real output, not using your mockery with exit codes and grepping for parts of the documents and
   output, do real job, do not assume, do not guess, verify and validate every fact you see."*
2. *"cut a new lab and run all rows again, from both vSphere VMs photon and ubuntu"* — **both OSes,
   both scenarios, six rows.**
3. *"After every lab cut, verify `make creds-show` / lab `make creds` endpoints and credentials
   ACTUALLY work — hit them, authenticate, drive the web UI in Chrome where an HTTP probe is not proof
   a human can log in."*
4. Read scenario-1 and scenario-2 **as a whole, as an end user**, executing line by line, observing
   full output, navigating **only** from the instructions below the commands just entered.
5. **Green FROM THE FIRST GO** — a fresh matrix over the FINAL tree passing on its FIRST pass. Every
   fix resets that clock. A row that goes green on a re-run after a fix is **not** a first-pass green.

### 1. What the six rows ARE (grepped from `nested-vsphere-lab/scripts/walk-matrix.sh`, not recalled)

| row | OS | lab state | doc | cut |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | ubuntu | NOTHING exists | scenario-1 | **A** |
| 2 | photon | EVERYTHING exists (what row 1 created) | scenario-1 | **A** |
| 5 | photon | EVERYTHING exists | **scenario-2** | **A** (runs LAST in A) |
| 3 | photon | NOTHING exists | scenario-1 | **B** (after the rebuild) |
| 4 | ubuntu | EVERYTHING exists | scenario-1 | **B** |
| 6 | ubuntu | EVERYTHING exists | **scenario-2** | **B** |

Cut A → **rebuild the lab** → cut B. Rows 5 and 6 are the *same* scenario-2 document on *different
OSes*; they are not a duplicate and neither substitutes for the other.

### 2. What rows 5 and 6 specifically require

Both are gated on `supply_s2_contract`, which must hand the row **ADMIN credentials AND a
driver-supplied GUEST kubeconfig — NOT the tenant path**. If it cannot, the row is
`UNRUNNABLE_ROWS`, **never walked**, and that is *not* a pass and *not* a failure: it is *no result*.
Reporting an UNRUNNABLE row as anything other than "never walked" is a false claim.

Their failures land in `S2_FAILED_ROWS`, tracked **separately** from `FAILED_ROWS`, because a
scenario-2 refusal must never gate cut B's rebuild.

### 3. Knobs (the complete set `walk-matrix.sh` reads)

`WALK_ROWS` · `WALK_SCENARIOS` · `WALK_CLUSTER_NAME` (+`_EXPLICIT`) · `WALK_SKIP_REBUILD` ·
`WALK_SKIP_CLONE` · `WALK_CLONE` · `WALK_REPO` · `WALK_RUN_ID` · `WALK_OUT_ROOT` (default
`/tmp/walk`, mode 0700) · `WALK_OUT_DIR` · `WALK_DNS_ATTEND_SECONDS` · `WALK_TKR_WAIT_SECONDS` ·
plus the lab/credential set: `VCENTER_HOST`/`VCENTER_USERNAME`/`VCSA_SSO_PASSWORD` ·
`VKS_NAMESPACE`/`VKS_USERNAME`/`VKS_SSO_DOMAIN`/`VKS_CONTEXT_NAME`/`VKS_STORAGE_POLICY` ·
`HARBOR_URL`/`HARBOR_USERNAME`/`HARBOR_PASSWORD`/`HARBOR_STORAGE_CLASS` ·
`ARGOCD_NAMESPACE`/`ARGOCD_PROJECT` · `VCF_CLI_SRC_DIR` · `LAB_ROOT`/`LAB_STATE_DIR`.

⚠️ `WALK_ROWS`'s default has **already shipped wrong once** (`"1 2 3 4"`, covering **neither**
scenario-2 row — nvl #65). Pass rows explicitly; never trust the default to mean "all".

### 4. Execution discipline — what "do real job" forbids

- **Read the whole document as an end user.** Not the blocks the harness extracted — the prose
  between them is the navigation, and a reader who cannot get from block N to block N+1 by reading
  it has found a defect even when every command succeeds.
- **Observe FULL, REAL output.** Not `rc=$?`. Not `| grep -c`. Not a tail. The verdict of a block is
  what it printed, read.
- **No exit-code proxies.** A completion notification's "exit code 0" is the LAST command's status
  and is a documented liar here (`; echo`, `| tail`, a cancelled run, a timeout-cancel). Never
  report pass/fail from it.
- **Verify and validate every fact.** A fact recalled is a claim; a fact grepped, fetched, or run is
  a measurement. Grade them differently and say which you have.
- **Do not assume, do not guess.** If a step cannot be settled, say it is unsettled and name the one
  command that would settle it.

### 5. After every lab cut — credential verification (item 3, in full)

`make creds-show` (repo) and lab `make creds` must be **exercised, not printed**: hit every endpoint,
authenticate for real, and **drive the web UI in Chrome** wherever an HTTP probe is not proof a human
can log in. A 200 from `curl` is not a login. A cert error in Chrome is a *trust* finding, not a
reachability finding — distinguish them and say which.

### 6. Security constraints — in force for the whole run

- **Secrets never in argv.** `curl -K` config under `umask 077`, `--data @-` on stdin, or env-by-name.
- **The vCenter SSO account LOCKS OUT AFTER 3 FAILED ATTEMPTS.** Never brute a password, never retry
  a failed auth blind.
- **Do not type passwords into web login forms.**
- **`harbor-robot.env`, `gitea-ci-token`, `webhook-token` must NEVER be blanket-deleted** — they are
  the only copy in existence.
- Walk logs carry live credentials: `/tmp/walk` and every per-run dir are **0700**, and a row log is
  citable only with that in mind.

### 7. Measurement discipline — how a result is read

- **The verdict is the per-invocation file**, `${WALK_OUT_ROOT}/run-${WALK_RUN_ID}/VERDICT-${WALK_RUN_ID}.txt`.
  Never a fixed path (a stale log lies), never stdout scrollback, never the exit code.
- **Read every row's own `WALK DONE` / `DOCUMENT` lines** plus the **"N of 6 designed rows"**
  denominator. `MATRIX FAILED` is the harness's wording for *did not complete successfully* — rows
  can reach `WALK DONE` and still be red.
- **`FAILED` and `UNMET` are different**: a failed block is a command that broke; an UNMET Expect is
  the document promising something the run did not print. Report both counts per row.
- **Do NOT edit `walk-matrix.sh` (or any script) while a matrix runs** — bash reads a script
  incrementally, so a rewrite mid-run yields `unexpected EOF` that reads as a product bug.
- **Kill the process GROUP, never the pid** (`setsid` + `kill -- -PGID`), and read `etime` after any
  kill. An orphaned driver has rebuilt a lab for 25 minutes while every later symptom was blamed on
  the lab.
- **A row's red is a hypothesis until root-caused.** Half of a matrix's reds have been the harness,
  not the product — and half of those "obviously harness" ones were real bugs. Prove which.

### 8. Rule Zero still applies

Adversary-review a **design** BEFORE implementing it, and again on the **diff** after. A fix to a
scenario doc or a script is a design. `isolation: "worktree"` on every dispatch.

### 9. Reporting

Times in **EST/EDT**. Commit and push roughly every 30 minutes. Report only: what shipped, what is
verified, what is still unknown.

| **B130** | ⚠️ **REFUTED (idea-round, `vks-adversary`, 2026-08-17) — the premise was FALSE and the fix would have remedied a failure that does not occur.** This row claimed the missing `osConfiguration` stanza means `make vks-cluster-create` builds a cluster that CANNOT PULL FROM HARBOR. I measured the ABSENCE of the stanza and asserted a PULL FAILURE — two different claims, joined by an unearned "so". **MEASURED, and it already works:** `scripts/40-install-gitea.sh:30` pulls Gitea FROM HARBOR into the GUEST cluster and `:62` BLOCKS on `rollout status --timeout`; Harbor's cert is privately issued (`CN = Harbor CA`, scenario-1.md:520); and the Timings table records `install-all` completing in **10 m 26 s** and **8 m 14 s** with `verify` green — on a tree with **zero** `osConfiguration` (`grep -rn osConfiguration scripts/ k8s/` = 0). An x509 failure would have timed that rollout out. It did not. **THE FREE WIN, now taken:** harbor.md's same-Supervisor auto-trust row was graded `community — verify on a lab` while the flow already DEPENDED on it and passed; promoted to **lab-verified** with the evidence chain, which `docs/lab-validation-plan.md:463` had already specified as the action. **FOUR MORE REASONS NOT TO BUILD IT:** (1) the CA does not EXIST at create time — script numbering encodes it, `25`-vks-cluster-create runs before `27`-harbor-ca-from-cluster — so a mandatory `secretRef` names an absent Secret on rows 1 and 3 every time; not circular (Harbor exists at §4) but a walkthrough REORDERING. (2) the `--dry-run=server` gate CANNOT catch it — an absent Secret is a cross-object existence question, not a schema one — so it would land 4–9 min later inside `vks-cluster-status`, the worst place. (3) `envsubst` has no conditional, so the stanza is in EVERY render; moving it script-side voids this row's own "mechanical coverage" argument by taking the YAML out of `k8s/` where yamllint and `check-cluster-template-vars` see it. (4) a TENANT never runs `make vks-cluster-create` — scenario-2 §3c is explicitly the `kubectl edit cluster/<name>` path — so the change helps only the scenario-1 admin, the one persona measured not to need it. **IF the capability is ever wanted** (defensible for a Harbor on a DIFFERENT Supervisor, which the evidence above does NOT cover): a post-create `kubectl patch` target guarded on an explicitly-set env var — rows 1/3 byte-identical, no template change, no reordering. Four lines, not thirty-six. **Residual, unmeasured:** whether admission accepts a `secretRef` to an absent Secret is INFERRED, not measured — it is the single highest-value lab check if this is ever revisited. |

The original FATAL came from a run where `install-all` died **before** reaching `platform`; on any run that gets that far the token exists. So this row's fix A (*add `make seed-gitea` to the documented sequence*) would have appended a command to a sequence that already runs it, and fix B (*say why a tenant skips `verify`*) would have documented a restriction that does not exist. **The row was right to say "decide which, do not just append a command"** — and the answer was neither. ⚠️ Three backlog prescriptions were refuted on 2026-08-17 alone (B130, B37, B67); acting on this one without waiting for the discriminator would have been a fourth. **The same block DID surface a real defect** — `install-all` failed at `gitops` with `x509: cannot validate certificate for 192.168.101.131 because it doesn't contain any IP SANs`, reported to the operator as an AppProject/RBAC question. | **B124** | 🟡 **A doc comment that ENUMERATES a make target's prerequisite chain is unverified, and one was two steps stale.** MEASURED 2026-08-17 while answering "what can scenario-2 reuse from scenario-1": `docs/scenario-1.md:731` described `make install-all` as `preflight -> mirror -> builder -> platform -> gitops` while `Makefile:793` is `preflight mirror mirror-verify builder-image vks-login platform gitops` — **two steps missing, one misnamed**, and the missing `mirror-verify` is the integrity gate this repo calls load-bearing (a push you have not verified is not a mirror; `crane` establishes blob existence with a HEAD, so a registry that answers HEAD-200 for blobs it cannot serve makes the whole push a silent no-op). `docs/scenario-2.md:599` had it right, which is the only reason it was caught — two documents describing one target differently means at most one is correct. **FIXED in #687**, verified token-for-token rather than by eye. **The GAP that remains:** `check-doc-make-targets` proves every `make <target>` a doc names EXISTS (299 of them across 31 docs), and **nothing proves a described PIPELINE is real**. A gate could compare any `# a -> b -> c` comment beside a `make X` against `X`'s actual prerequisite list. ⚠️ **NOT BUILT ON PURPOSE** — inventing a new control is the act that most needs an idea-round first, and this one has obvious false-positive surface (a comment may legitimately abbreviate, may describe a subset, or may name user-facing phases rather than make targets). Design it, RED-prove it against #687's own before-state, and measure its false-RED rate over the real doc corpus BEFORE shipping. |

| **B123** | ✅ **CLOSED (#685 + #686).** Both fixed; the class was scanned (8 pipelines across the three docs, 5 correctly guarded, these 2 not) and the round REFUTED my first fix — `[ -s "$CA" ] || { echo …; }` returns 0 and was still a fake green; the shipped form is `b64=$(base64 -w 0 "$CA") && printf … | base64 -w 0`, rc=1 absent / rc=0 present. Original text: **Two `scenario-2` blocks have an UNGUARDED pipeline exit status, and they fail in OPPOSITE directions — one is a FAKE GREEN, one is a silent RED. Both are in row 5's path.** Found 2026-08-16 by reading the document line by line as an end user, not by grepping; every number below was RUN. **(a) `:430` — FAKE GREEN.** The block is `base64 -w 0 harbor-ca.crt \| base64 -w 0`, a **bare** path — while the same document says the file is `./secrets/harbor-ca.crt` at **`:211`** (the `.env` key table) and **`:292`** (Step 2 prose), and nothing `cd`s. Measured in a dir without the file: prints `base64: harbor-ca.crt: No such file or directory` and exits **`RC=0`** without `pipefail` (**`RC=1`** with; walk-doc sets no `pipefail` — grepped, 0 hits). So the reader pastes an **empty** value into their Cluster `trust.additionalTrustedCAs` and the walk scores the block a **PASS**. It is invisible to the harness by construction. Fix: read the document's own key — `CA="${HARBOR_CA_FILE:-./secrets/harbor-ca.crt}"` — and make the absent-file case LOUD. ⚠️ Check `-w 0` against **toybox** `base64` before shipping: row 5 runs on Photon. **(b) `:475` — silent RED.** `if [ -s "$SUP" ]; then kubectl … get pods -A \| grep argocd-application-controller; else echo …; fi` has **THREE** outcomes, not the two the prose at `:479-480` claims (*"Either branch tells you what you need"*): kubeconfig absent → echo → **rc=0**; present **and** argocd found → **rc=0**; present and **NOT** found → `grep` exits 1 → the whole `if` returns **rc=1** and **prints nothing at all** — the reader is told neither what happened nor what to do, and the walk reddens the block. Measured both non-obvious cases directly. Fix: give the no-match case its own message and its own exit status; the block is informational, so it should never be able to fail the walk. **The CLASS, scanned:** 7 pipelines live inside runnable fences across both documents; **5 are correctly guarded** (`scenario-1:532` `\|\| true` + a `$code` check; `:542` guarded by the `[ ! -s ]` outcome test B119 added; `scenario-2:129` captures stderr and prints it at `:133`; `:130`/`:131` check `${VAR:-NOT FOUND}`) and **these 2 are not**. Do not fix only the instances — the shape is *"a command whose failure is the non-final element of a pipeline"*, and neither `check-doc-expect-leak` nor any gate in `static-check-fast` can see it. |

| **B64** | 🔴 **§2's "browser work — not scriptable" is a claim about THIS REPO presented as a claim about the PLATFORM — and the obvious port to fix it was REFUTED (idea-round, `vks-adversary`, 2026-08-05).** The source repo (`nested-vsphere-lab`) installs Supervisor Services entirely over vCenter REST, so the doc claim is false. But the proposed ~1,700-line port (a `lib/vcenter.sh` shim + `service-catalog.sh` + an `svc_main` equivalent, phased ArgoCD-instance-first) was refuted on 12 findings, 3 CRITICAL. **(1)** Blanking `_SS_ROWS` field 6 (`dns-label`) does not drop DNS — it feeds `svc_dns_fqdn`, so it kills `_ss_render_harbor_values` AND `harbor_set_admin_password`, dying with an error naming `.lab.domain`, **a config key this repo does not have**. Blank field **5 only**. **(2)** The install POST/PUT need `_SS_MOID`, which comes from `_cluster_moid` in the source's `steps/40-supervisor.sh` (not `lib/govc.sh`), plus an entire vCenter credential surface this repo lacks — `VCSA_IP`/`VCSA_USER`/`VCSA_SSO_PASSWORD` (**SSO administrator**, not the tenant identity Scenario 2 assumes), `CLUSTER_NAME`, `DOMAIN` (we have `APP_DOMAIN`), `LAB_STATE_DIR`. ~10–15 new `.env.example` keys, each owing a graded `# how:`. **(3) SECURITY:** with no VMCA anchor producer here, `_vc_tls` falls back to `-k`, so the **SSO admin password crosses an unverified TLS connection** on every first run, signalled only by two WARNING lines in a 20-minute build. The source's warn-and-continue exists for a bootstrap ordering (the VCSA does not exist yet) that **does not apply here** — vCenter is pre-existing customer infrastructure, so this repo must **fail closed**. **PHASING WAS BACKWARDS:** the "kubectl-only" ArgoCD-instance phase depends on the vCenter phase in four places, incl. a `die` telling the operator to go do the browser flow — the very claim being refuted. **COUNTER-PROPOSAL (adopt):** ONE script, four calls — `checkContent` → `register` (`carvel_spec`) → `install` (POST/PUT) → poll `config_status` — parameterised on (service-yaml, data-values, cluster). No catalog, no DNS, no instance CR, no teardown verbs (~800 lines serving a story this repo does not have). ~350–450 lines, it IS the §2 rewrite, and it keeps the vCenter-admin blast radius small enough to RED-prove. **Also carry:** the expose-key precondition verbatim (`yq -e 'has("enableNginxLoadBalancer") and has("enableContourHttpProxy")'` — `yq '.k = v'` CREATES missing keys, so without it a vendor rename ships a Harbor with no reachable ingress, green all the way down) — but note it has a **multi-document hole** (`has()` passes if ANY doc satisfies it; measure `select(di == 0)` against the real licensed artifact first). **Gates it will trip:** `check-grep-q-pipe` (the ported `printf \| grep -qxF` is the SIGPIPE-under-pipefail class), `check-env-coverage`, `check-how-provenance`, `check-env-clobber` (new selectors must stay COMMENTED), `check-doc-target-coverage`. **Doc honesty:** §2 is scripted through step 4 and **manual at step 5** — customer DNS is unscriptable from here, and claiming otherwise just mirrors the error being fixed. **OWNER DECISION NEEDED before writing:** does vCenter **SSO admin** belong in a repo whose Scenario-2 persona is explicitly a tenant? **Cheap unblock:** one `openssl s_client -connect $VCSA:443 -showcerts` against a real lab settles whether `./secrets/supervisor-ca.crt` already anchors vCenter too, which would delete a whole fetch path.  **SECOND ROUND (2026-08-05, `adversary-bash-git-cli`, Step-0 instrument PERMITTED — findings MEASURED): verdict SPLIT — the `checkContent`-only slice is CLEARED as not-worth-building, but on a stronger argument than the one I gave, and the DELIVERABLE was REFUTED.** **(F1, the decisive argument — lead with it)** `checkContent` is call 1 of 4 and NON-MUTATING: it cannot register, cannot install, and cannot move §2 off the browser. The operator gains a script demanding a new vCenter endpoint, a session handshake and a TLS anchor, and then still performs all six §2 steps by hand — the full credential surface of the four-call script for ZERO capability. "It validates the wrong file" invites "then also add the data-values check"; "call 1 of 4 delivers no capability" is unanswerable. **(F2, CRITICAL)** A doc correction MUST NOT close this row. The recorded owner action (`docs/reviews/2026-08-04-first-real-lab-measurements.md:56`) is verbatim *"port the capability, then rewrite §2/§3 scripted-first … This is a FEATURE plus a doc rewrite, not a doc edit."* Closing B64 on a doc edit also orphans **B78**, which is blocked on it, and reads six weeks later as "B64 was handled". Ship any §2 correction explicitly labelled **INTERIM**; B64 stays 🔴. **(F3)** This row's own "no VMCA anchor producer" claim is **CORRECT — do not "correct" it.** MEASURED: `command grep -rn 'supervisor-ca.crt' Makefile scripts/` returns ONE hit, a COMMENT, and `scripts/lib/tls.sh:87` says verbatim *"Nothing in the repo re-fetches it"*; `.env.example`'s `VKS_CA_CERT_FILE` is COMMENTED, i.e. a path, not a producer. A proposed edit to soften it was refuted. **This is also the strongest argument FOR refusing the slice:** a fail-closed `checkContent` at §2 hard-blocks the FIRST scripted step on an artifact the docs never tell anyone how to obtain, and the obvious bootstrap (fetch the VMCA over the connection you are verifying) is TOFU. **(F4)** The COST objection is OVERSTATED — drop it. MEASURED as already present: `VKS_USERNAME` (documented as `administrator@vsphere.local`, which IS the vCenter SSO principal), `VKS_SSO_DOMAIN`, `VCF_CLI_VSPHERE_PASSWORD`, `SUPERVISOR_HOST`, `VKS_CA_CERT_FILE`/`VKS_CA_SHA256`, and a RED-proven fail-closed `ca_verifies_endpoint`. Genuinely new is the **vCenter endpoint** (`SUPERVISOR_HOST` is the Supervisor VIP, not vCenter), and `check_content` needs only `System.Read`. The "~10–15 new keys" figure belongs to the four-call script, not this slice. **(F5, UNMEASURED)** a candidate consumer exists at `docs/scenario-1.md:259` and was never checked. ⚠️ **BLOCKED ON B133 — an unrecorded dependency, found 2026-08-17.** This row's finding (3) is that the port makes THIS repo send the vCenter **SSO administrator** password over a `-k` connection on every first run, and concludes it *"must fail closed"*. That is not a detail of the port — it IS B133, whose two pending OPERATOR decisions are exactly (1) whether Step 2 hard-fails on a box with no vCenter CA (breaking every current operator and every walk row) or keeps sending the password unverified, and (2) whether scenario-1 may be REORDERED so a vCenter CA is obtained first. Implementing B64's counter-proposal introduces the very credential surface those decisions govern, so starting it before they are made would either bake in the insecure default or break the walk. **Do not pick this up as 'the highest-severity open row' — it is not startable.** B78 (*the §4a/§4b consumer*) is blocked on B64 and is therefore blocked on B133 too. 🟢 **THE 'CHEAP UNBLOCK' IS MEASURED, 2026-08-17, on a FRESH cut — and the answer is YES BY KIND, which does delete a fetch path, but it came with a second finding that matters more.** This row proposed: *one `openssl s_client -connect $VCSA:443 -showcerts` settles whether `./secrets/supervisor-ca.crt` already anchors vCenter too, which would delete a whole fetch path.* Run against the new lab's vCenter (192.168.100.50): **(1) vCenter presents exactly ONE certificate** — leaf `subject=CN = vcsa.env1.lab.test, C = US`, `issuer=CN = CA, DC = vsphere, DC = local, … O = vcsa.env1.lab.test, OU = VMware Engineering` (the **VMCA**), `SAN=DNS:vcsa.env1.lab.test`, and it does **NOT** send its issuer — `Verify return code: 21 (unable to verify the first certificate)` against the system store. So the *read-the-chain-off-the-wire* shortcut is **unavailable**: an anchor must come from somewhere else, which is why `make trust-vcsa` exists and why every build prints *"no VMCA anchor … talking to vCenter with TLS VERIFICATION OFF"*. **(2) `secrets/supervisor-ca.crt` IS the VMCA root** — `subject == issuer == CN = CA, DC = vsphere, DC = local, … O = vcsa.env1.lab.test`, i.e. **the very DN that issued vCenter's leaf**, not a Supervisor-specific CA. ⇒ on a MATCHING cut that one file anchors BOTH the Supervisor and vCenter, and the separate vCenter-CA acquisition path this row worried about can be elided. ⚠️ **(3) AND THE MEASUREMENT ACCIDENTALLY DEMONSTRATED THE STALENESS HAZARD, WITH A MISLEADING ERROR.** The `supervisor-ca.crt` on disk is dated **Aug 16 12:55** — the DESTROYED cut — so against the new vCenter it FAILS: `curl --cacert` gives **rc=60 http=000** (insecure control: rc=0 http=200), and `openssl verify` reports **error 20, *unable to get local issuer certificate*** — NOT error 7 (signature failure). Same subject DN, different key, so the anchor is never even considered a candidate and the diagnostic says *no issuer found*, which reads as **"wrong KIND of CA"** when the truth is **"right kind, wrong cut"**. That is the B161/B133 family exactly: a stale credential-adjacent artifact surviving a rebuild and producing an error naming the wrong cause. **RESIDUAL, stated rather than hidden:** this compared a STALE anchor to a NEW vCenter. The full claim — that a MATCHING `supervisor-ca.crt` verifies vCenter — needs the pair from ONE cut, and settles the moment this build's Supervisor is up: `make fetch-supervisor-ca && curl --cacert secrets/supervisor-ca.crt https://<vcsa>/` must return **200**. |
| **B61** | **A MEASURED-FALSE fact sits in the ALWAYS-LOADED rules corpus — `claude-config:rules/common/git-workflow.md:377-379` (other repo, but it loads into EVERY session here).** It claims *"`$(...)` capture is also not word-split in zsh"*. **Measured FALSE** (zsh 5.9, `zsh -f`, `SH_WORD_SPLIT` unset): `for x in $(printf 'a\nb\nc\n')` → **3** iterations — command substitution DOES split; `v=$(...); for x in $v` → **1** — only *variable* expansion does not. Removing a false fact is a **correction** — the ratchet docstring names that always-legitimate — and it SHRINKS the corpus, so it also partly repays the 627 B raise of 2026-07-23. The line now reads *"an unquoted VARIABLE expansion runs ONCE in zsh — a `$(...)` capture DOES split; measured 1 vs 3, zsh 5.9 `-f`"*, which I RE-MEASURED independently on zsh 5.9 with `-f`: iterating a command substitution of three lines gave **3 iterations**; assigning it to a variable first and iterating that gave **1**. The corpus is correct as it stands. (2) The "~29 B of mangled duplication" is **NOT mangled** — lines 386-387 are the inline code span `` `while read` `` legitimately wrapped across a line break. ⚠️ **AND MY OWN INSTRUMENT MANUFACTURED THAT SECOND FINDING**: I first read the region through a viewer that truncated each line at 170 columns, which cut line 386 mid-span and removed the opening `` `while ``, leaving a dangling closing backtick before *"from a file"* that looked exactly like corruption. Reading the raw bytes dissolved it. The lesson is the deliverable: **a TRUNCATING viewer is an instrument**, and a defect visible only through it belongs to the instrument, not the file — read raw bytes before reporting a file corrupted. (3) Closing this by READING rather than by a status grep is exactly what this file's own prose above demands; a keyword census would have left it open, since it carries no closure marker. |

An `items` guard now exists, but ⚠️ **it does NOT close corpus vacuity and cannot be made to** (its own source supplies items; measured floor **372** under maximal starvation — an items-based vacuity guard is unfireable by construction). It guards **TOTAL FOLD DEATH ONLY**: with `sed` shimmed to exit 127 the gate previously reported `scanned 125, items 0, rc=0` — green over zero examined lines. ⚠️ **That RED-proof is now STALE and the sed case is caught EARLIER**, by the fourth positive control's emptiness arm — a later change preempted the guard it was written for, which is why the control's message names sed explicitly instead of blaming the expression. `items` also does **not** prove the loop READ THE FILES: with `"$@"` dropped from `_fold` it read the heredoc's FILE LIST and reported `scanned 1, items 125` while missing a planted violation, with all four controls green. | **B52** | ✅ **CLOSED 2026-07-19 — the sweep was REFUTED BY MEASUREMENT, and it was aimed BACKWARDS. Do NOT reopen it as a conversion sweep.** The row proposed converting ~70 bounded `printf '%s' "$var" \| grep -q` sites. An idea-round adversary refuted BOTH halves of its premise, and I re-measured every number independently. **(a) "not safe in principle" is FALSE — the safety boundary is STRUCTURAL, not a hope.** Below the 64KB pipe buffer the producer's write COMPLETES and SIGPIPE cannot occur (**4KB: 0/120**); above it, it always does (**82KB multiline: 120/120**); the transition is narrow (41KB: 4/120, 61KB: 3/120). **And data with NO NEWLINES is immune at ANY size — 3MB single-line: 0/120** — because grep buffers hunting a line terminator and thereby drains the producer. Risk therefore needs **>32KB AND multiline**, and **not one** `printf` site qualifies. **(b) "the remaining are bounded printf sites" was ALSO false**, and this is the part that mattered: **six were EXTERNAL producers whose size we do not control** — `kubectl get deploy -A -o name \| grep -q .`, `docker ps -a --format \| grep -qxF`, `kind get clusters \| grep -qxF` — and the gate waved every one through, because none is a file reader. **The sweep would have churned 30 files to remove a structurally-zero risk while leaving the only real ones untouched.** ⚠️ **The `<<<` conversion is itself a hazard, which is the second reason not to sweep:** `printf '%s' "$v"` on an EMPTY value emits zero bytes (rc=1) while `<<< "$v"` emits ONE EMPTY LINE, so every nullable pattern (`''`, `^$`, `.*`, `^`, `$`, `x*`) flips rc 1→0. Several of those sites are `if`-conditions in TESTS, where that flip is a silent verdict change — a false green in a test. (`printf '%s\n'` is identical to `<<<`, so those few are free; the DRAIN form `\| grep PAT >/dev/null` has no empty-var divergence at all and is what shipped.) **WHAT SHIPPED INSTEAD:** the six unbounded sites converted to the drain form, and `check-grep-q-pipe` widened with an `_EXTERNAL='kubectl\|docker\|kind\|helm\|crane\|curl\|argocd\|tkn\|podman'` producer class (RED-proven in three directions: a kubectl form caught, a docker form caught, a bounded printf form still ALLOWED — no false positive). Their POLARITY is why it was worth doing: `48-istio-preflight.sh:39` inverts with `!`, so a SIGPIPE there reports **"no istiod"** when istiod EXISTS; `05-kind-up.sh:55` reports **"cluster absent"** and re-creates it; `kind-down.sh:23` **skips the sidecar cleanup**. ⚠️ ✅ **Residual SETTLED 2026-07-20 on a live, fully-loaded cluster** (argocd + harbor + gitea + tekton + istio + both apps): `kubectl get deploy -A -o name` = **941 bytes / 27 deployments**; `docker ps -a --format '{{.Names}}'` = **174 bytes**. Both are **nowhere near** the measured 32KB risk threshold — ~35 B per deployment, so you would need roughly **900 deployments** to reach it. So those six conversions were **DEFENSIVE, not urgent**, and nobody should cite this row as evidence that a SIGPIPE was actually occurring here. The polarity argument for making them still stands unchanged (a SIGPIPE at `48-istio-preflight.sh:39` inverts `!` and reports "no istiod" when istiod EXISTS), and the external-producer class remains the right thing to gate — it is only the urgency that was overstated. The fix is cheap and correct regardless. |
| **B90** | 🔴 **The walk box resolved a STALE Harbor A record that the lab's DNS attendant had ALREADY fixed — "verified" is verified from the wrong vantage.** MEASURED 2026-08-12, row 1: `/tmp/walk/dns-r1.log` ends `nested-mgmt: harbor.env1.lab.test -> 192.168.101.139` / `supwl: … -> .139` / `verified: … resolves to 192.168.101.139`, while the walk box at `04:29:29Z` resolved **192.168.101.136** (block `[11]`) and was still on `.136` at block `[13]` (135 s, `000` — see B89). ⚠️ **CORRECTED 2026-08-17 — this row described the vantage WRONGLY, and the correction matters.** It said the attendant "verifies from its own vantage (nested-mgmt + supwl)". Read `nested-vsphere-lab/scripts/dns-record.sh`: `:57` prints `${net}: ${NAME} -> ${IP}` **once per network** — those are the two PUBLISH lines, not verifications — and `:62-66` verifies exactly once, with `getent hosts` **from the HOST running the harness**. So there are THREE vantages, not two: the two dnsmasq instances it writes to, the HOST it reads back from, and the WALK BOX, which nothing checks. Getting this wrong points a fix at the networks when the gap is host-vs-walkbox. **PARTIALLY ADDRESSED 2026-08-17 (nvl):** the message now says `verified FROM THIS HOST: … (a walk box resolves separately and is NOT checked here — B90)`. That is a false-confidence fix only — it removes the misreading that cost row 1, and changes no behaviour. The row STAYS OPEN for the real fix. The record moved `.130 → .133 → .134 → .135 → .136 → .139` across reinstalls — every Harbor reinstall mints a new LB IP — so this is **structural, not incidental**. ⚠️ The mechanism (resolver cache on the box vs. update-after-query ordering) is **NOT established** — do not claim which until it is measured. **Fix lands in `nested-vsphere-lab`'s harness** (verify the record FROM the walkbox, and/or flush its resolver, before letting the walk proceed), not here. ⚠️ **SHARPENED 2026-08-17 — the attendant is STRUCTURALLY INCAPABLE of noticing this, and rows 5/6 have NO attendant at all.** Read `walk-matrix.sh:559-575`: `dns_attendant` is a LOOP (20 s poll, `WALK_DNS_ATTEND_SECONDS` 5400 s), so my first reading — *the box is built at `:626` before the attendant is launched at `:644`* — is TRUE but NOT the cause; the loop would fix a late publish within 20 s. The cause is its TRIGGER: `:567` reads `cur` with `getent hosts` **on the HOST**, and it republishes only `if [ "$cur" != "$ip" ]`. So in the exact state B90 measured — HOST already correct at `.139`, BOX stale at `.136` — the condition is FALSE, the attendant does nothing, and its own log line is true of the host and false of the box. It cannot see the vantage that matters, by construction. **AND `:643-648` disables it entirely for scenario-2** (a deliberate, correct choice: publishing an A record is an ADMIN act, and a tenant row that silently fixed the shared Harbor's DNS could never discover it was broken) — so for **rows 5 and 6 a stale box resolution is wholly unmitigated.** ⚠️ **NOT FIXED BEFORE RUN 4, on purpose:** changing DNS/attendant behaviour immediately before a six-row certification run risks all six rows to fix one, and the standing bar is green FROM THE FIRST GO. Watch for it instead — a row failing against a Harbor IP that the host resolves correctly is this bug. 🔬 **MECHANISM NARROWED 2026-08-17 — the RESOLVER-CACHE candidate is REFUTED for this lab, which changes the fix from a FLUSH to a BARRIER.** This row forbade claiming a mechanism until measured; here is the measurement, taken on cut B from all three vantages. **The walk box does run a caching stub** — `systemd-resolved` **active** on `nameserver 127.0.0.53`, `nsswitch hosts: files dns`, and **0** `/etc/hosts` entries for the name, so resolution is pure DNS through a cache-capable resolver. That is what makes the cache hypothesis plausible. **But the record is served with `TTL 0`** — measured three ways: through the stub, and directly against BOTH lab resolvers (`dig @192.168.100.1` and `@192.168.101.1` each return `harbor.env1.lab.test. 0 IN A 192.168.101.130`). A TTL-0 answer is **not cacheable**, so there is no staleness window on the walk box to flush — and the remedy this row floated first (*flush its resolver*) would fix nothing. ⇒ the surviving candidate is the other one it named, **update-after-query ordering**: the walk resolved before the attendant's write had taken effect. That implies the fix is a **BARRIER** — verify the record FROM the walk box (poll until it matches the expected IP) before letting the walk proceed — which is the other half of what this row proposed, and is the half that actually applies. ⚠️ **LIMITS, stated rather than glossed:** (1) all three vantages AGREED at measurement time (host `.130`, walk box `.130`, ground truth `.130` — `https://192.168.101.130/` answers 200 with title `Harbor`, while `.136` and `.139` from the original incident are both dead), so this measures the MECHANISM's preconditions, not a reproduction of the divergence; (2) `TTL 0` is what dnsmasq serves **on cut B today** — the incident was 2026-08-12 on a different cut, and I did NOT verify the TTL was 0 then, so a cache could in principle have applied to that cut; (3) `resolvectl statistics` printed nothing readable here, so the *no cache hits* arm is **INCONCLUSIVE** — the refutation rests on the TTL, which is a mechanism argument, not on observed counters. Anyone implementing the barrier should re-measure the TTL first: if a future dnsmasq config serves a non-zero TTL, the cache candidate returns and the fix needs BOTH halves. 🟢 **TTL RE-MEASURED 2026-08-17 on a FRESH cut, which is the check this row's own tail demands before anyone implements the barrier.** The tail says: *"re-measure the TTL first: if a future dnsmasq config serves a non-zero TTL, the cache candidate returns and the fix needs BOTH halves."* Measured against the lab's own resolver while the new lab was building: `dig +noall +answer @192.168.100.1 esxi01.env1.lab.test` → `esxi01.env1.lab.test. **0** IN A 192.168.100.51`. **TTL is ZERO**, and it is zero by DEFAULT rather than by configuration — grepping all three libvirt dnsmasq conf files (`default.conf`, `nested-mgmt.conf`, `supwl.conf`) for `local-ttl=`/`max-ttl=`/`no-negcache` returns **nothing**, so dnsmasq's built-in `local-ttl` of 0 is what is in force. ⇒ **the cache candidate does NOT return**, and the refutation's TTL mechanism argument now rests on a measurement of THIS cut rather than on inference. ⚠️ Two scope limits, stated rather than hidden: (1) this is the DEFAULT, so it holds only until someone adds an explicit `local-ttl` — the row's conditional is still the right thing to re-check on any dnsmasq config change, and this measurement is the baseline to compare against; (2) `harbor.env1.lab.test` returned **no answer** at measurement time, which is EXPECTED and not a finding — the lab was mid-build and Harbor is not installed until later, so do not read that as a DNS defect. (b) `podman save --compress` → **not supported** for docker-archive (fails). (c) gzip the podman tarball → **285,575,559 B** (i.e. ~97% of docker's win, podman retained) but requires a `gunzip` step + transient disk in `22-builder-push.sh` **on the air-gap box**. (d) prefer docker in `14-builder-build.sh` → 287 MB with no decompress step, but the artifact's shape then depends on which engine the internet box happens to have, and it pushes against the **"docker is opt-in, never required"** invariant that `test-container-engine.sh` check 7 enforces. ⚠️ **The genuinely useful half of this row is NOT the size** — it is that `test-builder-save-crane.sh`'s honesty block claimed both engines default to the SAME format and **that was false**; the claim survived because a successful `crane push` proves crane handles **both** and says nothing about them being the **same**. The discriminating command was `tar tf`. That correction is already in the script's header and in `rules/common/testing.md`. |

Also unverified [`community`]: the prompt **recurs at token refresh**, so a long `make install-all` can block mid-run — which is the argument for exporting it for the session deliberately rather than scoping it to `context create` alone. |

| **B75** | 🟢 **CLOSED 2026-08-17 by MEASUREMENT — correct AS SCOPED, INCOMPLETE as a class (see B137, filed one hour later when the matrix produced a counter-example at a SIBLING site). The prescribed work was already shipped and then some; the one real residual (an UNPINNED arm) is closed here.** I did not take the row's word for it; I checked each of its five prescriptions against the code and ran the suite. **(1) extend, do not fork** — exactly **1** definition of `classify_kube_failure` (`lib/os.sh`). **(2) anchor `401` AND move it below the network arm** — both done, and the code carries the measurement inline (60 invocations against a refused endpoint with no `401` in the address; **4 of 60** contained `401` from klog microsecond fields like `.380401` and classified UNAUTHORIZED while the other 56 classified UNREACHABLE — a heisenbug a rerun 'fixes'). **(3) kubectl's real expiry phrasing** — present. **(4) PLAINTEXT bucket** — present. **(5) 'all 7 sites across 5 files'** — now **18 sites across 11 files**, so coverage expanded well past what the row asked. The row's first HIGH (`argocd account can-i` discarding the x509-vs-RBAC discriminator, since both are rc=1) is **gone**: `grep -rn 'account can-i' scripts/*.sh` = **ZERO**. The second (a stale kubeconfig CA reported as 'unreachable') is distinguished at 5+ consumer sites via `STALE_CA`. **THE ONE THING THAT WAS ACTUALLY MISSING**, and a grep of the row would never have found it: the arm that catches kubectl's TRANSLATED 401 — its stderr contains neither `Unauthorized` NOR `401`, so it reads *error: You must be logged in to the server (the server has asked for the client to provide credentials)* — had **ZERO test cases**. The arm existed, was correct, and nothing would have gone red if someone deleted it. Two cases added (both phrasings, because the arm matches EITHER and an edit could drop one while the suite stayed green) and **RED-PROVEN in a sandbox: deleting the arm turns exactly those 2 cases red — `want UNAUTHORIZED, got UNKNOWN` — and leaves the other 18 green**, so they pin that arm and nothing else. Suite 18 → **20 passed, 0 failed**. The arm is load-bearing precisely because it is complementary, not redundant: kubectl's translated 401 carries NO numeric token, so the anchored-`401` arm below it cannot catch it — drop either and a real 401 classifies UNKNOWN. **Original row preserved below as the record.** ——— ORIGINAL ——— **The rebuild-staleness class is NOT closed, and the two paths that WERE fixed are the two whose consumers did the checking.** Two HIGH: a TLS-trust failure against argocd-server is reported as a PERMISSION denial (`argocd account can-i … >/dev/null 2>&1` — x509 and RBAC are both rc=1 and the discriminator is discarded), and a stale kubeconfig CA is reported as "the cluster is unreachable" when the cluster is up. Round verdict **ship-with-changes** with three corrections: **`argocd account can-i` EXITS 0 ON A DENIAL** (rc files a refusal under "permitted" — cited from upstream pinned to the installed v3.4.5); `91-e2e-tenant-mechanism.sh`'s RED-1 is a **negative control that passes vacuously** on any transport failure; and **redact-before-classify CHANGES the verdict** (measured: a 1-char secret turned STALE_CA into UNKNOWN). Also: the `401` arm collides with kubectl's **klog microsecond timestamp** — 4/60 = 6.7% non-deterministic on ANY kubectl failure — and, being arm #2, SHADOWS FORBIDDEN. And `harbor-tls` carries a **byte-identical `ca.crt`** (same fp) while its `tls.key` is only the LEAF's. ⚠️ **CORRECTED 2026-08-16, AND THIS ROW HAD ALREADY DELETED THE CORRECTION ONCE — do not delete it again.** An earlier version of this row called the swap *"a real least-privilege win"*. **That is wrong, and B83-orig BELOW already said so** — verbatim: *"the exposure is the **RBAC GRANT**, not the read — k8s has no field-level authz on Secrets, so anyone who can run the script can already read `tls.key` themselves; the jsonpath constrains what the SCRIPT writes, never what the OPERATOR can read."* Writing this row on 2026-08-16 dropped that sentence, so the newer entry read as current truth and was the *less* accurate one — the `configuration.md` §"PRUNING a doc DELETES THE CORRECTION" shape, at row granularity. **The honest claim: the swap reduces EXPOSURE (on the happy path the CA signing key never crosses the wire and never enters kubectl's memory), which is a PRECONDITION for a least-privilege grant — it is not the grant.** A privilege win would require a `Role` with `resourceNames: ["harbor-tls"]` plus the doc line telling a platform admin to grant that; no such Role exists anywhere in the repo. ⚠️ **Finding (b) is HALF WRONG, and the half that is right still matters:** the row says `harbor-tls` *"does NOT reliably carry `ca.crt`"* — on this **VKS-installed** Harbor it DOES (keys measured: `['ca.crt','tls.crt','tls.key']` on both secrets). KinD is therefore **already dead at rung 3**; hardcoding `harbor-tls` would move it from *"dies at rung 3: extracted ZERO bytes"* to *"dies at rung 2: no `ca.crt`"*. **Both die — there is no KinD regression for the ladder to prevent, so that argument must not be used to justify it.** 🔴 **WRONG RIGHT NOW, INDEPENDENT OF THE LADDER, and the cheapest thing here — 4 sites point the operator at the WRONG SECTION.** Verified: `## 6.` is **Guest cluster**, `## 8.` is **Harbor's CA**; yet `27-harbor-ca-from-cluster.sh:8`, `:13`, **`fetch-ca.sh:116` (an operator-facing `die`)** and **`Makefile:556` (`make help`, a copyable artifact)** all say **§6** for the Harbor CA. Also correct this row's reason for *"KinD is dead at rung 3"*: **measured**, `06-install-harbor.sh` sets `appplatform.vmware.com/serviceId` ZERO times so label discovery cannot reach KinD — TRUE — but `:55` is an escape hatch that bypasses discovery, so what actually makes KinD unreachable is the **Supervisor-kubeconfig requirement at `:49-51`**, not the label. ➕ **NEW, FOUND BY THE B66 ROUND (2026-08-17) — filed here because it is Harbor-install, not B66's install-vs-consume: `04-install-harbor-service.sh:110-117` guards REGISTER for idempotency but calls `vc_ss_install` UNCONDITIONALLY.** `vc_ss_register` is wrapped in `if vc_ss_is_registered ...; else ... fi`, but the `vc_ss_install` on the next line has no already-installed check and POSTs with `$VALUES` — which on a wiped `.env.state` (lab rebuilds are routine here) carries **freshly generated secrets**. ⚠️ **The CONSEQUENCE is UNVERIFIED** — what vCenter does with a repeat install against a live Harbor was not measured, and it may well be a benign upsert. **Do not write code before measuring it**; it is a vendor-behaviour question, which is exactly the class this repo requires to be measured rather than inferred. If a fix is needed it is an already-installed check in `04`, not a mode flag (an admin re-running their own installer would never set one). ⚠️ **ITS SETTLING COMMAND IS ANSWERED 2026-08-17, on a real lab — BOTH halves, and both favour the swap.** Namespace `svc-harbor-rk6ra` (note: VKS names it `svc-harbor-<id>`, NOT `harbor` — my own measurement script's `^harbor` grep missed it on the first pass and reported *no harbor namespace*). **(1) MECHANISM:** BOTH secrets carry `cert-manager.io/*` annotations — `harbor-tls` from issuer `harbor-ca-issuer` (`cert-manager.io/certificate-name: harbor-tls-cert`, alt-name `harbor.env1.lab.test`) and `harbor-ca-key-pair` from issuer `harbor-self-signed-ca-issuer` (`certificate-name: harbor-ca`, alt-name `harborca`). Per the row's OWN verdict key, `cert-manager.io/*` ⇒ **a MECHANISM produces the equality; the swap is sound**. NO `kapp.k14s.io/versioned` on either, so the name-churn finding does NOT fire. **(2) ROOT vs INTERMEDIATE:** `harbor-tls`'s `ca.crt` is `subject=CN = Harbor CA`, `issuer=CN = Harbor CA`, `X509v3 Basic Constraints: critical CA:TRUE` — a **self-signed ROOT**, so `ca_anchor_reject_reason` will not refuse it and the swap is NOT an availability regression. Both conditions the row named as blocking are therefore cleared. 🔴 **DO NOT BUILD — finding (a) CLOSED 2026-08-17 by an idea-round, and the round REFUTED MY BRIEF AND THIS ROW'S OWN NEWEST CORRECTION.** ⚠️ **THIS ROW'S `:49-51` CLAIM IS MEASURED FALSE — that is the THIRD time this row has made itself less accurate by editing, and it is live misinformation until read.** The row asserts KinD is unreachable because of the Supervisor-kubeconfig requirement; measured, `supervisor_kubeconfig()` (`lib/os.sh:713-714`) ends its candidate list with **`"${KUBECONFIG:-}"`** and `05-kind-up.sh:104` does `state_set KUBECONFIG`, so that guard **PASSES** on KinD. The only delta is which bytes transit into kubectl's memory and are discarded, and the operator who can run the script can already `kubectl get secret harbor-ca-key-pair -o jsonpath='{.data.tls\.key}'` in one command. **AND THERE IS NO KinD ASYMMETRY** (my brief's premise, refuted): KinD dies at the SAME line with the SAME message before and after. **The code is a FALLBACK inside a collapsed `<details>`** (`scenario-1.md:618-624`), which `walk-doc.sh:552` excludes — so no walk row, no e2e and no gate executes it, and `test-env-file-mode.sh:93` only greps for a chmod. **A change nothing runs is a change nothing can certify.** The privilege win remains unbuildable as scoped: `git grep resourceNames` over `k8s/ docs/ scripts/` = **0 hits**, so without a `Role{resourceNames:["harbor-tls"]}` plus the doc line telling a platform admin to grant it, capability is unchanged — that is a DIFFERENT, larger row needing its own round and an owner decision. **(e) is CLOSED as stale:** all four §6→§8 pointers are already correct (`27:8`, `27:13`, `fetch-ca.sh:116` all say §8; `Makefile:556` carries no section; `scenario-1.md:532` is `## 8. Harbor's CA`) — verified. | **B83-orig** | 🟠 **`27-harbor-ca-from-cluster.sh` reads the wrong Secret, and accepts a LEAF as a trust anchor.** Now the FALLBACK (scenario-1 §6 leads with `curl .../api/v2.0/systeminfo/getcert`, MEASURED 2026-08-09: http 200, 1155 B, `CN = Harbor CA`, sha256 identical to the trusted CA — no login, no Secret read). Findings from `adversary-security-secrets`, all measured: **(a)** the script reads `harbor-ca-key-pair`, whose response also carries `tls.key` — **the CA's signing key**, which mints a valid cert for ANY name every consumer trusts, offline, until 2036. `harbor-tls`'s `ca.crt` is byte-identical (measured, `cmp` identical) and its `tls.key` is only the leaf's. ⚠️ **Settle first**: if `harbor-tls`'s `tls.crt` == its `ca.crt`, that secret is ALSO a CA keypair and the swap is cosmetic — one `openssl x509 -noout -subject -issuer` decides it. **(b)** `harbor-tls` does NOT reliably carry `ca.crt`: `kubectl create secret tls` produces only `tls.crt`/`tls.key` (measured), and **`06-install-harbor.sh:202` creates this repo's own `harbor-tls` that way** — so prefer a LADDER (the `tkg.tanzu.vmware.com/tkg-registry-additional-ca-cert` Cluster annotation from B70, which carries NO key material at all → `harbor-tls` → `harbor-ca-key-pair` with the 🔴 warning printed at point of use), never a hardcoded name. **(c)** line 64 only checks the blob PARSES as a certificate — it accepts a **leaf**. Harmless today only because `ca.crt == tls.crt` in the keypair secret; reading `harbor-tls` makes a one-token jsonpath slip install a leaf as the anchor and exit 0. Add `subject == issuer` + `basicConstraints CA:TRUE`. **(d)** `scripts/test-*.sh` has **ZERO** coverage of this script. **(e)** three other sites name the secret, incl. `fetch-ca.sh`'s operator-facing `die` (shared with ArgoCD — keep it generic). | ✅ **(c) and (d) SHIPPED 2026-08-13.** The anchor check is now a pure `ca_anchor_reject_reason` in `lib/tls.sh` requiring self-issued **and** `basicConstraints CA:TRUE`, with a 10-case offline test (`test-ca-anchor-validation`, wired into `test-scripts`) — closing (d)'s ZERO coverage. ⚠️ **My first fix was itself defective and an `adversary-security-secrets` round MEASURED it:** it matched `*CA:TRUE*` against the whole `openssl -text` dump, so three self-signed `basicConstraints=critical,CA:FALSE` certs carrying the literal in a CN, an O and a **subjectAltName URI** were all ACCEPTED — the SAN vector being the one that matters, since every real cert has a SAN. It now reads `-ext basicConstraints` (a closed output set; already relied on at `02-env.sh:444` and `30-vks-login.sh:193`, so zero portability delta). Same round refuted `openssl verify -CAfile <f> <f>` as an alternative — MEASURED **OK** for a self-signed CA:FALSE cert — and found the function validated only PEM block #1 of a file the caller installs whole (now refuses a multi-cert file). **(a)/(b) REMAIN, and (a) is not deferred so much as ORDERED SECOND:** a ladder that can fall back to `harbor-tls` is only safe once a leaf is rejected, which is what (c) added. The settle-first command needs the lab: `kubectl -n <harbor-ns> get secret harbor-tls -o jsonpath='{.data.tls\.crt}' | base64 -d | openssl x509 -noout -subject -issuer -ext basicConstraints`. ⚠️ **My stated reason for deferring (a) was FACTUALLY WRONG** — I claimed a naive swap would break the KinD path; measured, `27-...sh` cannot reach KinD at all (it needs a Supervisor kubeconfig and selects by a label `06-install-harbor.sh` never sets). Right conclusion, wrong evidence. The real mechanism: the exposure is the **RBAC GRANT**, not the read — k8s has no field-level authz on Secrets, so anyone who can run the script can already read `tls.key` themselves; the jsonpath constrains what the SCRIPT writes, never what the OPERATOR can read. **CLASS: 1 of 5 anchor-installing sites adopted.** Next is `fetch-ca.sh:121`'s MULTI-CERT branch (takes 'the last (the issuer)' with no self-issued and no CA:TRUE test); NOT changed here because refusing an intermediate is a real behaviour change for a server that legitimately presents leaf+intermediate, and that needs its own decision. |

| **B135** | ✅ **CLOSED 2026-08-17 — ALREADY FIXED IN #770, and the scoping this row proposed was REFUTED by that PR's own round.** **`.env` is 0664 while the credential it holds is 0600 everywhere else.** Independent of B132, pre-existing. `22-harbor-robot.sh:171-179` is meticulous about `secrets/harbor-robot.env` — `rm -f` first, `umask 077`, 0600, seven lines of comment — and twenty lines later writes the SAME secret to `.env`, whose mode `set_env_var` PRESERVES (measured: **664**). `.env.state` is 600. Fix separately so the two stay independently revertable. ⚠️ **RE-MEASURED 2026-08-17 — the defect is REAL, but the row's '664' is a SYMPTOM OF ONE BOX and states the cause too narrowly.** On this box `.env` is **600**, not 664 — so the row's headline does not reproduce, and someone re-checking it would wrongly conclude it was already fixed. The actual defect is that **`set_env_var` never ENFORCES a mode**; it does `touch "$file"` and inherits whatever the ambient umask gives. Measured by driving the real function into a temp dir: **umask 022 → 644**, **umask 002 → 664** (the row's number — and this box's umask IS 002), and a **pre-existing 0644 file written under umask 077 stays 644**. That last case is the one that matters most and no umask can fix it — the corpus rule is *"umask protects a file only at CREATION; a pre-existing world-readable file KEEPS its mode"*. So the honest statement is: **the protection of the file holding the Harbor robot credential depends on the ambient umask of whoever first created it, and a loose mode is never repaired.** `.env` being 600 here is luck, not a guarantee. **The fix must chmod explicitly, not rely on umask** — and it should be decided together with which sinks `set_env_var` legitimately writes, since it takes the file as a required argument. ⚠️ **MEASURED by an idea round commissioned FROM THIS STALE ROW (its brief was a verbatim copy of this row's text).** `scripts/lib/os.sh:971` already reads `chmod 600 "$file" 2>/dev/null \|\| true`, placed exactly where this row's Q3 asks for it — before any content is written — via commit `83425e4` / **#770** (*"the credential store was born 0664, and the admin password never touches set_env_var"*). It ships with a gate, `scripts/test-env-file-mode.sh`, measured **6/6**. **AND THE FIX I WAS ABOUT TO PROPOSE — "have `set_env_var` enforce the mode" — is the exact scoping #770's OWN round refuted**, in its words: *"`set_env_var` is not where the mode is decided. `env_init`'s `cp` births the file at 0664, and scenario-1 tells the operator to HAND-EDIT `VCENTER_PASSWORD` into it at Step 2 while the first hardening writer does not run until Step ~5."* The shipped fix touches **three** sites (`set_env_var`, `02-env.sh` `env_init`, `state.sh` `state_unset`). **Two answers worth keeping:** the container path does not merely tolerate 0600, it **REQUIRES** it — `jumpbox-run.sh:17-37` deliberately excludes every `.env*` from the `/src` copy *because* it cannot read them, and that exclude is now DERIVED (`find`), not enumerated, after the rename-rot incident; and the cost is nil — **2000 `chmod` calls = 1038 ms** against a function that already forks `mktemp`/`grep`/`cat`, with no TOCTOU because the window between `touch` and `chmod` holds a **zero-byte** file. |

Both are artifacts of the question. The head-scan misses closures appended at the TAIL, which is exactly where every closure in this file lands; the last-marker scan picks up a 🔴 belonging to QUOTED ORIGINAL TEXT further down the same cell. **There is no regex that reads these rows**, because a row is a long append-only prose history with historical verdict markers embedded throughout, and the current verdict is identified by MEANING, not position. ⚠️ **So do not report a backlog count as if it were measured, and do not build a better classifier** — that is the trap this row exists to name. ('make argocd-preflight' checks exactly that.)"* — an AppProject/RBAC question for a transport failure, plus a recommendation to run a tool that measures RBAC and can never surface the cause. `70-configure-argocd.sh` (the Application-creation path, `Makefile:636 configure-argocd`). ⚠️ **THIS CORRECTS MY OWN CLOSURE OF B75, MADE ONE HOUR EARLIER.** B75's first HIGH was *"a TLS-trust failure against argocd-server is reported as a PERMISSION denial"*, cited at the `argocd account can-i … >/dev/null 2>&1` site. That measurement was right and the closure was **correct-as-scoped and incomplete-as-a-class**: the INSTANCE was removed, the CLASS recurred at a sibling site, and the matrix produced the counter-example within the hour. Fixing one instance is not fixing the class — and I have the receipt. **ROOT CAUSE IS B68, now doubly measured:** ArgoCD's cert carries NO externally-valid name and NO IP SAN — on this cut its SANs are `localhost, argocd-server, argocd-server.cicd, argocd-server.cicd.svc, argocd-server.cicd.svc.cluster.local` — and this path dials it by **IP**. B68 records that `argocd.<domain>` answers 200 *"solely because every caller passes `--insecure`"*; this caller does not, so it hits the wall B68 describes. **AND THE LADDER CONTRADICTED ITSELF ONE LINE EARLIER:** the probe correctly said *"the argocd API probe FAILED TO ANSWER (STALE_CA) — this is NOT a denial. argocd-server never received the request, so no AppProject role will change it. Not selecting the api mechanism on a capability nobody measured"* — and the very next line reads `write mechanism: api  (kubectl=no, argocd-api=unknown)`. Either the ladder falls through to `api` as a last resort despite that sentence (then the sentence is misleading) or it is a genuine bug; **settle which before designing a fix.** **THE FIX IS NOT A BETTER MESSAGE ALONE** — `classify_kube_failure` already distinguishes STALE_CA and the probe already used it correctly; the defect is that the FAILURE PATH of the Application create discards the CLI's own x509 text and substitutes a guess. Capture the CLI's stderr, classify it, and say TLS when it is TLS — and do not recommend `argocd-preflight` for a transport fault. Evidence: `/tmp/walk/run-20260817T061515Z-1427190/MATRIX-row5-photon.log:2586-2601`. ✅ **THE BLOCKING QUESTION IS SETTLED, AND THE ANSWER IS NEITHER HYPOTHESIS THIS ROW OFFERED.** The row said: *either the ladder falls through to `api` as a last resort (then the sentence is misleading) or it is a genuine bug; settle which before designing a fix.* Measured in the source: the `unknown`-guard that dies (`70-configure-argocd.sh:289-294`) lives **only in the `auto` arm**, and `api) MECH=api ;;` (`:278`) bypasses the whole `case`. `ARGOCD_MECHANISM=api` is set **EXPLICITLY** by `docs/scenario-2.md:264` and by the harness at `nested-vsphere-lab/scripts/walk-matrix.sh:393`, and the `auto` die text appears nowhere in the row-5 log. So the ladder is not buggy and the sentence is not misleading *on the `auto` path* — the tenant path simply never reaches either. ⚠️ **AND MY OWN DIAGNOSIS OF THE REMEDY WAS FALSE, refuted by an idea round and then by my own measurement.** I claimed a name mismatch means *the anchor is irrelevant; fetching the CA cannot help*. Measured with go1.26.5 and a real TLS handshake (`/tmp/x509probe`): a leaf carrying DNS SANs only, dialled by IP, emits `x509: ... doesn't contain any IP SANs` **IDENTICALLY whether the CA is trusted or not** — Go verifies the HOSTNAME BEFORE the chain, so a name fault **MASKS** the anchor. The anchor is not irrelevant, it is **UNMEASURED**, and a message saying *do not re-fetch the CA* would strand the operator on a second round trip. **SHIPPED (commit `14b3712`):** (1) four measured x509 shapes added to `test-classify-kube-failure.sh` — two of the arm's three tokens had **ZERO** coverage (deleting `*"x509"*` AND `*"certificate is valid for"*` previously left **20 passed / 0 failed**; RED-proven in a sandbox, the same mutation now gives **20 passed / 4 FAILED**); (2) a guard refusing up front when `MECH=api` and the probe answered `unknown`, naming the reason already held 160 lines earlier — the run used to create a namespace, apply PSA labels and mint an image-pull secret **before** dying with a guess; (3) `argocd app create` **and** `argocd app wait` now CAPTURE and CLASSIFY the CLI's stderr. `app wait` was **worse** than the site this row found: it sent stderr to `/dev/null` outright and then blamed repo-server/Gitea, a different component. ⚠️ **NO NEW CLASSIFIER VERDICT, deliberately.** The round measured **7** case-form consumers, not the 15 my file-grep suggested — and **all 7 are kubeconfig sites**, where the anchor (`certificate-authority-data`) and the address (`cluster.server`) travel in ONE file so both faults have the identical remedy. A split would turn 7/7 RED for a remedy delta of **0/7**, while `70-configure-argocd.sh` — the one place the anchor and the address are independent knobs — does not call the classifier at all. **RESIDUAL:** `argocd repo add` (`~:551`) is still unclassified, and the walkbox `/etc/hosts`→`argocd-server` route (a bare SAN that measurably gives FULL verification with no `--insecure`) is not built. ✅ **CLOSED 2026-08-17 — VERIFIED ON `main`, not taken from the task list.** The fix landed in **#719** (`fix(argocd): a TLS transport fault is no longer reported as an RBAC denial (B137)`), and `main` now carries **7** transport-aware lines. Measured with no explicit `KUBECONFIG`, three keys were stripped from a sink stamped for another cluster and `state_check` never refused. **Right predicate: "was this sink SOURCED in this process"** — have `load_env` export `_VKS_STATE_SOURCED=1` inside its `if state_check` branch and `0` otherwise; `state_unset` warns and returns **0** when it is `0`; **unset ⇒ proceed** (no change for callers that never ran `load_env`). Archiving here re-creates the read-path hazard `state_check` was written to avoid. **Skip-with-warn, never archive.** |

The Application's `spec.source.repoURL` is exactly that URL on `main`, with **zero** error conditions. 🔴 **AND "NEVER FETCHED" IS FALSE.** The same Application now reads `sync=Synced health=Healthy operationPhase=Succeeded`, carries a deploy HISTORY (`21:14:42, 21:15:50, 21:38:41, 21:39:46`), and **reconciled again at 22:02:47Z — about EIGHT MINUTES after our 21:54:20Z FATAL**. `gowebapp`, the control on the same path, is also Synced/Healthy. So the run gave up on a convergence that then happened on its own. ⚠️ **THE ARITHMETIC IS THE TELL, and it makes this a race BY CONSTRUCTION rather than bad luck:** our wait is **180s** and ArgoCD's default reconcile poll is **~3 minutes**. We wait almost exactly ONE poll interval, so a single missed poll is a guaranteed FATAL. **Graded `measured` for the refutation, `inferred` for the poll-interval mechanism** — I did not read this instance's `timeout.reconciliation` from the argocd-cm. **FIX (design, needs its own round):** (a) wait a MULTIPLE of the reconcile interval, and READ that interval from `argocd-cm` rather than hardcoding a number that happens to equal it; (b) FORCE a refresh instead of waiting for the poll — `kubectl annotate application <app> argocd.argoproj.io/refresh=hard` is the mechanism `git-workflow.md` already records for exactly this ("wait for the deployed IMAGE to change, NOT a generic Synced/Healthy"); (c) **stop naming a cause the check has not measured** — if it is going to say "most likely X", it must first PROBE X (the clone handshake from the repo-server is one exec away and returns a status code). That last point is B163's finding arriving a second time in one run, in a different file. ⛔ **Do NOT "fix" this by raising 180s to a bigger number** without (a) or (b): a longer arbitrary wait is the same race with a wider window, and it makes every genuine failure slower to report. | 🔴 **MY DIAGNOSIS IS REFUTED BY ITS OWN IDEA ROUND — 2026-08-17, and the refutation came from the code I had not read.** **(1) MY FIX (b) IS ALREADY IN THE TREE**: `scripts/70-configure-argocd.sh:770` annotates `argocd.argoproj.io/refresh=hard` **immediately before** the wait loop. Its presence refutes my claim 3 from the code alone — the run did NOT wait for a poll, it FORCED a refresh and still saw an empty field for 180 iterations, and no poll-interval story can explain a failure that happened after a forced refresh. **(2) CLAIM 3'S MECHANISM WAS WRONG ANYWAY**: a NEW Application is enqueued for refresh on ADD by the controller's informer, not on the next tick; `timeout.reconciliation` governs RE-reconciliation of an app that already has `ReconciledAt`. That my 180s equalled the 180 default is a **coincidence of two independently-chosen numbers** — precisely the kind that manufactures a confident wrong cause. **(3) MY CLAIM 2 WAS OVERSTATED BY ITS OWN EVIDENCE**: the deploys I cited (21:38:41 / 21:39:46) are BEFORE the wait window, which was **21:51:12Z-21:54:20Z** and is itself **UNOBSERVED**. So NotFound, Forbidden, a wrong namespace, a wrong kubeconfig and "no resource type application" all yield the IDENTICAL empty string, after which `:778-783` names `GITEA_ARGOCD_URL` — a variable that branch never probes. **PROVEN, not inferred:** in `MATRIX-row5-photon.log`, line **2626** prints *"could not clone the repo. Any fix ships with a fixture that sets `ARGOCD_SERVER`, omits `ARGOCD_LB_IP`, and asserts the URL contains **no** `--insecure` and no bare IPv4. **MEASURED ON THIS BOX 2026-08-17:** `grep -c '^ARGOCD_LB_IP=' .env.state` -> **0**, so the precedence bug is NOT firing here right now — but the KinD flow writes that key, so any box that has run `make kind-up` carries it. **ALSO NOTED:** `creds.sh:150`'s `mktemp` is not trap-guarded (LOW — mode is 0600, so untidy rather than a disclosure), and `creds.sh:151` is the concrete file:line for **B153**'s printer-side half — it prints the ArgoCD admin password read FRESH from the Secret, which is exactly the printer B153 says shape-keyed redaction cannot see. ✅ **CLOSED 2026-08-17 — MERGED in #801, verified on main by content:** `creds.sh:83` tests `ARGOCD_SERVER` first with `ARGOCD_LB_IP` in the `elif`, the `fetch-argocd-ca` recipe is inverted the same way, harness green. The KIND-stamp design I first proposed was REFUTED by a measured 5-state table (repairs 1 of 3, REGRESSES one), and `ARGOCD_LB_IP` was deliberately NOT dropped — its writer is Supervisor-only, so removing it breaks `make fetch-argocd-ca` on KinD. Four cases added where `ARGOCD_SERVER` had ZERO coverage across all six render fixtures, including a CONTROL proving KinD still works. **RESIDUAL, filed not hidden:** a THIRD pre-existing site — `creds.sh:251` trusts the same KinD stamp and thereby LAUNDERS PROVENANCE (measured: a mismatched overlay upgraded to `DISCOVERED`) — plus a NEW defect at `creds.sh:90-94`, which renders `https://Please enter Username:` when kubectl prompts on a credential-less kubeconfig (the same kubectl-prompt-to-stdout class as #800). Neither is fixed; both need their own round. |
| **B169** | ✅ **REFUTED 2026-08-17 — see the end of this row for the full arc.** **UNVERIFIED QUESTION, not a confirmed defect: in `INGRESS_CONTROLLER=istio` (INSTALL) mode with gateway-api routing, does `46-install-istio.sh` publish the CLASSIC gateway's address while the routes live on the AUTO-PROVISIONED one?** Found 2026-08-17 closing B38's residual. **Measured:** `46-install-istio.sh:216` sets `LB_IP="$(istio_wait_lb_ip)"` and `:220` publishes it; `istio_wait_lb_ip` (`lib/istio.sh:499,509`) reads `ISTIO_GATEWAY_NAMESPACE/ISTIO_GATEWAY_SERVICE`, which `46:37,53` set to `istio-ingress`/`istio-ingressgateway` — the **classic helm-installed** Service. So install mode applies classic VirtualServices and publishes the classic gateway's address — **internally consistent**, both classic. The row's premise (routes on the auto-provisioned gateway, address from the classic one) cannot arise because `istio_apply_routes_gwapi` is never reached on that path. **The live measurement in this row stands and does not contradict it:** `istio-ingress/istio-ingressgateway`=`192.168.101.134` → curl 56 vs `vks-ingress/vks-uis-istio`=`192.168.101.135` → 200 was observed in **ATTACH** mode (row 2 took `istio-existing`), which is exactly the mode that branches correctly. **Residual, checked and NOT a defect:** `ISTIO_ROUTE_API` is silently ignored in install mode — but `.env.example:735` documents it under the heading *"--- Istio: SOMEONE ELSE installed it (INGRESS_CONTROLLER=istio-existing) ---"*, so its scope is stated where an operator sets it. Nothing to fix; the combination this row wanted tested (install + gateway-api) **does not exist as a code path**, which is why no row exercised it. |

This adds **jq's alternative operator after a credential-named field**. **It is a REAL false positive, not a near-miss:** the matched text is a JSON path plus an operator, and the gate's own failure message says a value-less hit should get a NARROW allowlist. **WORKED AROUND** by rewording (docs-only; `scripts/` was frozen for the certification run) — so **the gate still fires on the next person who quotes jq**, and quoting jq is normal in a repo whose scripts use it. **FIX:** add one narrow line to the existing exclusion chain at `check-prose-secrets.sh:32-49`, matching a jq alternative applied to a credential-named field, with a comment naming exactly why — and **RED-prove it still catches a genuinely restated credential** (plant one in a scratch `.md`, confirm rc=1, remove). ⛔ **Do NOT widen the pattern instead.** The gate exists because gitleaks misses natural-language credentials, and this repo has already shipped three live plaintext passwords in a runbook that gitleaks reported clean. **The allowlist must stay narrower than the thing it protects.** |
| **B149** | ✅ **CLOSED 2026-08-17 — the fix is ON `main`, and I VERIFIED IT BY RUNNING IT, not by reading its comment.** 🟡 **`make deps`'s failure message PROMISES something `check-tools` does not do, and the promise is false on every WARM box.** FOUND 2026-08-17 by reading scenario-1 §1 as an end user and RUNNING it (not by a grep). `make deps` failed on my box (`apt-get` needs a tty for sudo; `deps-prereqs` Error 1), and printed verbatim: *the sentinel .deps-failed is set, so 'make check-tools' will tell you the toolchain is INCOMPLETE BECAUSE THIS FAILED - instead of sending you back here.* I then ran `make check-tools` exactly as §1 instructs. It printed **`all REQUIRED tools present.`** and said nothing about the failure. **MECHANISM:** `03-check-tools.sh:176` reads the sentinel, but that branch sits inside the **`else` of a missing-tools test** — so it is reachable ONLY when something is absent. On a box where every tool already happens to be present (a re-run, a warm dev box, a second walk on the same VM) `deps` can fail and `check-tools` reports a clean toolchain in total silence. **WHY IT MATTERS even though 'all present' is literally TRUE:** the operator was just told, by name, what the next command would say. It says the opposite, so they reasonably conclude `deps` succeeded. Same class as B119's misattributed §8 message and B120's false cause-list — an error message asserting something the code does not do. **NOT the same as 'the toolchain is broken'**: in this state it genuinely is not, which is exactly why the silence is the defect rather than the verdict. **MINIMAL FIX (needs an idea round — it touches a control's messaging, and inventing/altering a control is the act that most needs one):** either (a) surface the sentinel in the ALL-PRESENT path too, as a WARNING — *a previous `make deps` failed; the toolchain nevertheless looks complete, so the failure was in a part you do not need* — which keeps the promise honest without inventing a false red; or (b) stop `deps` promising what another target will print, and have it say what IT knows. Prefer (a): it is the only one that tells the operator the two facts they actually hold. ⚠️ Do NOT make the sentinel FAIL `check-tools` — the tools are present, and a red there would be a false negative on a working box, plus it would break every warm-box walk. **Housekeeping observed in passing:** the sentinel is gitignored (`.gitignore:74`) and `make deps` removes it on a clean run, so it is operator-local state, not repo state. 🔬 **MECHANISM RE-VERIFIED on the current tree 2026-08-17, with exact anchors so the fix lands in the right branch.** The row was written from a live run; this is the structural confirmation an implementer needs. `scripts/03-check-tools.sh`: the sentinel is read at **:176** (`if [ -f "${REPO_ROOT}/.deps-failed" ]`), and it sits inside the branch reached when tools ARE missing — that branch's inner `if/else` closes at **:186**, and **:187 `else`** opens the ALL-PRESENT path, which runs **:188-:199** and reads the sentinel **nowhere**. The two all-present messages are **:189** (`PRE-CARRY OK …`, when `$pending_carried` is non-empty) and **:195** (`all REQUIRED tools present.`) — and note there are **TWO** of them, so fix (a) must surface the warning in BOTH or it will be silent on the pre-carry box, which is precisely the air-gap operator who can least afford a false all-clear. `exit "$rc"` at **:201** is unaffected either way, which is what keeps fix (a) from inventing a false red (the ⚠️ in this row). So the row's diagnosis is accurate as written; only its single-message assumption needed widening to two. Still needs its idea round before implementing — this note changes nothing about that, it just means the round can start from verified anchors instead of re-deriving them. **Live A/B on this box, both directions:** with `.deps-failed` present and EVERY tool present — the exact warm-box state the row says was silent — `make check-tools` now emits **7 WARN lines** naming the sentinel, what `deps` installs that this gate never checks (`java maven kustomize go node`) and CONFIGURES (container-engine runtime, registry search paths), the `rm -f .deps-failed` remedy, and the air-gap caveat that the sentinel may have TRAVELLED with the repo copy. **Control:** with no sentinel, **0** such lines. And in BOTH runs the literal `all REQUIRED tools present.` is still printed exactly once — which is load-bearing and nearly got broken: `docs/scenario-1.md` carries that string as an **Expect:** literal, substring-matched, so rewording the message instead of ADDING lines would have turned every walk row UNMET. The mechanism the row diagnosed was right (`03-check-tools.sh:176`'s sentinel branch sits inside the `else` of a missing-tools test, unreachable when nothing is missing); the fix surfaces it a second time in the all-present branch rather than moving it. Also worth keeping from the fix's own comment: it deliberately does NOT say *"the failure was in a part you do not need"*, because under `set -euo pipefail` a failure in `deps`'s FIRST `pkg_install` aborts before crun and podman's unqualified-search-registries — so a reassured operator walks into a `podman build` that cannot resolve a short name. |

The two-step form the doc prescribes correctly exits 1.
